# Chernobyl S01E05 — hearing-room explanation / visible process reconstruction — visual evidence v0.1

## Source and scope

- Evidence ID: `CHERNOBYL-S01E05-HEARING-RECON-001`
- Source identity: season/episode is filename-supplied and not independently proven from picture; the local basename remains temp-only and is excluded from this public candidate.
- Selected contiguous envelope: `00:38:51.840–00:52:53.800`, frames `[F58296,F79345)`, PTS `[29847552,40624640)`, 25fps CFR, time base 1/12800.
- 205 end-exclusive contiguous shots; 204 internal visible cuts.
- Scope caveat: this is a selected contiguous envelope inside a broader visible hearing-room scene, not the full natural scene.
- Picture-only study. Burned subtitles excluded. Audio not auditioned; all sound is `SND-UNK`.
- Rights boundary: no source media, audio, still, keyframe, contact sheet, copyrighted script or long dialogue is included.

## Research question

How can a model-neutral Director IR represent a scene that retains a formal-room explanation anchor while repeatedly visualizing a separate multi-stage process, then expands into large physical imagery and returns to the formal room—without falsely deriving roles, technical truth, responsibility, chronology, reaction causes or sound from subtitles and story knowledge?

## OBSERVED

- The selected envelope opens on a complete warm wood/curtain room containing lectern, two wheeled red/blue block boards, long seating, raised desk and crowd.
- The sequence alternates warm wood/curtain room-like imagery with green-gray console-room-like imagery, industrial halls/catwalks, object/readout inserts, a fish tank/desk pair of inserts, and later dark flame/haze/debris-like imagery. Exact cross-cut location grouping remains INFERRED/UNKNOWN.
- Separate numeric inserts contain orange digits; S024 and S096 visibly change digits within their individual shots. Other inserts show circular indicators, a hand at switches, a hand under a clear cover above a red button, an illuminated red labeled block, papers, and board blocks handled by a lectern adult. Cross-cut display identity and value continuity remain UNKNOWN.
- From S170 the image scale moves from an elevated circular industrial surface undergoing a broad visible change through tight person fragments and separate dark light/haze/airborne-fragment views to a distant night plume and later haze-obscured console-room-like imagery.
- At S189 warm hearing-room-like imagery reappears. S200 is materially longer than neighboring shots and contains one adult turning/moving within its room view; S205 presents a complete warm room view before the cut to a corridor. Grouping these room/person appearances across cuts is INFERRED.
- Every accepted boundary and every shot interval is specified below.

## INFERRED

- The recurring hearing-like room functions as an explanatory/attribution anchor.
- Alternate industrial/console images function as a visualized process, and the S170–S188 passage functions as a scale/escalation peak.
- Readout/hand/face/group ordering is a candidate grammar for making a script-verified state progression legible.
- The long S200 and closing S205 are candidate deceleration/restoration devices after the physical-image peak.
- These are interpretations of visible organization, not proof of dialogue content, technical truth, responsibility or intended audience response.

## HYPOTHESIS

A model-neutral planner may be more robust if it represents cross-location explanation as explicit state-bearing beats and return anchors, with chronology and causality supplied only by the locked project-original script rather than inferred from wardrobe, subtitles or familiar plot. The four rules below remain single-source candidates pending same-trigger counterexamples.

## UNKNOWN

Character names and roles; relationships and authority; responsibility or guilt; exact technical principles; whether alternate views are past, present, simultaneous, imagined or illustrative; exact location/object identity; reaction causes and emotions; dialogue; all sound; lens/support/focus/lighting/effect method; audience effect; same-trigger contrary sources.

## Method and boundary rationale

- The local source basename remains in temp QA only and is excluded from the public candidate; season/episode identity is filename-supplied and not independently proven from picture.
- Container inspection: H.264 Main, 1920×1080 progressive, 25/1 frames per second, stream time base 1/12800; audio stream exists but was not auditioned.
- Selected contiguous edit envelope: `[F58296,F79345)` = `00:38:51.840–00:52:53.800`, PTS `[29847552,40624640)`, duration `00:14:01.960`.
- Left boundary: accepted hard cut from a prior seated-table view into a fresh whole-room view containing lectern, two color-block boards, long seating and raised desk. This is the first complete visual room/diagram anchor retained for the selected research problem.
- Right boundary: accepted hard cut from the established whole-room view into a new corridor/doorway scene. The corridor is excluded.
- The broader visible hearing-room scene begins before this selected research envelope; therefore this package is explicitly a **selected contiguous envelope**, not a claim that it captures the entire natural scene.
- Cut audit: decoded every video frame from 00:38:30–00:53:10, computed subtitle-excluding 160×90 gray-frame MAD and histogram change, retained 259 local-max candidates, and visually inspected paired `F-1/F` frames for every candidate. A second lower-threshold pass produced 39 additional within-envelope pairs; all 39 were visually checked and none showed a missed cut. Rapid display-digit changes, smoke/light variation, hand movement and fire/debris motion were rejected when no edit boundary was visible.
- Final result: 205 shots, 204 internal cuts, 206 accepted boundary rows including envelope start and end. Every shot interval is end-exclusive and contiguous.
- Each accepted shot was additionally reviewed on a three-sample sheet (near-start, midpoint, end-minus-one). Contact sheets, candidate pairs and extracted review frames remain local temporary media and are excluded from the public package.
- Burned subtitles were ignored. They do not prove dialogue, roles, technical principles, responsibility, chronology, reaction cause or edits.
- Audio was not auditioned. All sound, dialogue, music, room tone, alarm/machinery/impact cues, sound bridges and audio-led cut motives are `SND-UNK`.
- Exact lens, camera support, focus method, lighting method, VFX/effect method, cross-cut identity and intended audience response remain `UNKNOWN` unless visibly bounded.
